\mathbf{x}(t)\rightarrow\mathbf{x}_{d}(t)
.

The proof is given in Appendix
G.5
.

Last, we consider the momentum algorithm for nonlinearly parameterized systems

\ddot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}+\left(\beta\mathcal{N}(t)-\frac{\dot{\mathcal{N}}(t)}{\mathcal{N}(t)}-\dot{\mathbf{P}}(t)\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\right)\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}=-\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\mathbf{P}(t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t),

(80)

which admits a representation as two first-order equations,

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{v}}}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\mathbf{P}(t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t),

(81)

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}

\displaystyle=\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\mathbf{P}(t)\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right).

(82)

Proposition E.4
.

Consider the adaptation algorithm (
80
) with
\mathbf{P}(t)
given by (
73
),
\lambda(t)
given by (
75
),
\mathcal{N}(t)=1+\mu\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(t),t)\|^{2}
, and
\mu>\frac{4D_{2}-2+\left(2D_{1}+1\right)^{2}}{\beta\left(4D_{2}-1\right)}
. Suppose
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)
satisfies Assumptions
2.2
and
E.1
. Further assume that
D_{2}>\frac{1}{2}
. Then, all trajectories
(\mathbf{x}(t),\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t))
remain bounded,
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
,
s(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{2}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
,
s(\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\rightarrow 0
and
\mathbf{x}(t)\rightarrow\mathbf{x}_{d}(t)
.

The proof is given in Appendix
G.6
.

Remark E.2
.

As in Remark
E.1
, because
\mathbf{P}(t)
is uniformly bounded in
t
, it is not necessary to include
\mathbf{P}(t)
in (
82
). It is simple to show by modification of the proof that

\ddot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}+\left(\beta\mathcal{N}(t)-\frac{\dot{\mathcal{N}}(t)}{\mathcal{N}(t)}\right)\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}=-\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\mathbf{P}(t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)

is also a stable adaptive law with a suitable choice of gains.

Appendix F
Proofs

F.1
Proof of Proposition
3.1

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{\theta}
be any constant vector of parameters. The Bregman divergence
d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)=\psi(\boldsymbol{\theta})-\psi(\hat{\mathbf{a}})-\nabla\psi(\hat{\mathbf{a}})^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)
has time derivative

\frac{d}{dt}d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)=-\left(\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\psi(\hat{\mathbf{a}})\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right).

From (
23
),
\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\psi(\hat{\mathbf{a}})=-\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
, so that

\frac{d}{dt}d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)=\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right).

Integrating both sides of the above shows that

d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}(0)\right)=d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)\right)+\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\tau),\tau)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\tau)-\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)d\tau.

By the assumption of a linear parameterization,
\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)=\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\frac{\partial Q}{\partial\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{x},t)
. If we now take
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A}
,
\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\tau)\boldsymbol{\theta}=f(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\mathbf{a},\tau)
and the integral term becomes independent of
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. Assuming that
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)\rightarrow\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}\in\mathcal{A}
, we can take the limit as
t\rightarrow\infty
and say that for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A},\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}\in\mathcal{A}
,

d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}(0)\right)=d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}\right)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial Q}{\partial\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\tau)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\tau)\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\tau)-f(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\mathbf{a},\tau)\right)d\tau.

Because the only dependence of the right-hand side on
\boldsymbol{\theta}
is in the first term, and because this relation holds for any
\boldsymbol{\theta}
, the
\arg\min
of the two Bregman divergences must be identical. The minimum of the right-hand side over
\boldsymbol{\theta}
is clearly obtained at
\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}
, while the minimum of the left-hand side is by definition obtained at
\arg\min_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A}}d_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{\theta},\hat{\mathbf{a}}(0))
. From this, we conclude that

\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}=\arg\min_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A}}d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}(0)\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

F.2
Proof of Proposition
3.2

Proof.

The proof is much the same as Proposition
3.1
. The Bregman divergence
d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)
for any fixed vector of parameters
\boldsymbol{\theta}
verifies

\frac{d}{dt}d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)=\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(t),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t),\mathbf{a},t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(t),t)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right),

so that, integrating both sides,

d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}(0)\right)=d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)\right)-\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t),\mathbf{a},\tau)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\tau)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\tau)\right)d\tau.

Now take
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A}
. By the assumptions of the proposition,
\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\tau)^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\theta}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\tau)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{a}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. Hence, using that
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)\rightarrow\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}\in\mathcal{A}
, we can write

d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}(0)\right)=d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}\right)-\int_{0}^{\infty}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\tau),\mathbf{a},\tau)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\tau)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\mathbf{a}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\tau)\right)d\tau.

Optimizing both sides over
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A}
as in Proposition
3.1
yields the result.
∎

F.3
Proof of Proposition
4.1

Proof.

By adding and subtracting
\mathbf{f}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})=\mathbf{Y}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})\mathbf{a}
, the error
\mathbf{e}=\hat{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbf{x}
has dynamics

\dot{\mathbf{e}}=-k\mathbf{e}+\mathbf{Y}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})\tilde{\mathbf{a}}+\mathbf{f}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})-\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x}).

Consider the parameter estimator

\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}=-\gamma\left[\nabla^{2}\psi(\hat{\mathbf{a}})\right]^{-1}\mathbf{Y}(\hat{\mathbf{x}})^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{e},

where
\gamma>0
is a constant learning rate,
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a strongly convex potential function, and
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}>0
is a constant symmetric positive definite matrix. Now consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\mathbf{e},\hat{\mathbf{a}})=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{e}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{e}+\frac{1}{\gamma}d_{\psi}\left(\mathbf{a}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}\right).

Let
\frac{\partial g}{\partial\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2})
denote the derivative of
\mathbf{g}
with respect to its first argument evaluated at
(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2})
.
V(\mathbf{e},\hat{\mathbf{a}})
then has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{e},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\left(\mathbf{g}\left(\mathbf{e}+\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}(t)\right)+\mathbf{Y}\left(\mathbf{e}+\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\tilde{\mathbf{a}}+\mathbf{f}\left(\mathbf{e}+\mathbf{x}(t)\right)-\mathbf{f}\left(\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\mathbf{e}+\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{e},

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\left(\mathbf{g}\left(\mathbf{e}+\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}(t)\right)-\mathbf{g}\left(\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}(t)\right)+\mathbf{f}\left(\mathbf{e}+\mathbf{x}(t)\right)-\mathbf{f}\left(\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\right),

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}^{\mathsf{T}}\int_{0}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\left[\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}}{\partial\mathbf{x}}\left(\mathbf{x}(t)+s\mathbf{e}\right)+\frac{\partial\mathbf{g}}{\partial\mathbf{x}}\left(\mathbf{x}(t)+s\mathbf{e},\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\right]ds\mathbf{e},

(83)

where the second equality follows by application of
\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{0}
for all
\mathbf{x}
and the last equality follows by Hadamard’s Lemma. (
83
) shows that
\mathbf{e}(t)\rightarrow 0
as long as
\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})+\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{u}(t))
is contracting in the metric
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}
for any external input
\mathbf{u}(t)

(
Lohmiller and Slotine 1998
;
Slotine 2003
)
, i.e., if

\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})}{\partial\mathbf{x}}+\frac{\partial\mathbf{g}}{\partial\mathbf{x}}\left(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{u}(t)\right)\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}+\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})}{\partial\mathbf{x}}+\frac{\partial\mathbf{g}}{\partial\mathbf{x}}\left(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{u}(t)\right)\right)\leq-2\lambda\boldsymbol{\Gamma}

uniformly in
\mathbf{x}
for some contraction rate
\lambda>0
. To see this, assume that
f(\mathbf{x})+\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{u}(t))
is contracting for any external input
\mathbf{u}(t)
. Then

\dot{V}(\mathbf{e},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)\leq-2\lambda\mathbf{e}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{e}\leq 0.

Because
V(\mathbf{e},\hat{\mathbf{a}})\geq 0
for all
\mathbf{e}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}
, and because
\dot{V}(\mathbf{e},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)\leq 0
for all
\mathbf{e},\hat{\mathbf{a}}
, and
t
, this shows that
\mathbf{e}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
,
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
and that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}V(\mathbf{e}(t),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t))<\infty
exists, is finite, and is positive. Integrating both sides of (
83
) shows that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{e}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{e}\leq\frac{V(\mathbf{e}(0),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(0))}{2\lambda}

so that
\mathbf{e}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
. It is straightforward to show that
\dot{\mathbf{e}}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
, so that an application of Lemma
C.1
leads to the conclusion that
\mathbf{e}(t)\rightarrow\mathbf{0}
.
∎

F.4
Proof of Proposition
4.2

After subtracting the true dynamics
\dot{\mathbf{p}}
and
\dot{\mathbf{q}}
from the dynamics predictor, consider the decomposition of the error dynamics

\displaystyle\dot{\tilde{\mathbf{p}}}

\displaystyle=-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{Y}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)\tilde{\mathbf{a}}-k_{p}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right),

\displaystyle\dot{\tilde{\mathbf{q}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathbf{Y}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)\tilde{\mathbf{a}}-k_{q}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}+\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\mathbf{q})\right)+\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\mathbf{q})-\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right),

The Lyapunov-like function

V(\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{q}},\hat{\mathbf{a}})=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}+d_{\psi}\left(\mathbf{a}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)

then has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{q}},\hat{\mathbf{a}})

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left[-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{Y}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)\tilde{\mathbf{a}}-k_{p}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left[\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathbf{Y}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)\tilde{\mathbf{a}}-k_{q}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}+\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\mathbf{q})\right)+\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\mathbf{q})-\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{Y}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathbf{Y}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left[-k_{p}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left[-k_{q}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}+\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\mathbf{q})\right)+\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\mathbf{q})-\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathsf{T}}&\tilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathsf{T}}\end{pmatrix}\mathbf{M}(\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{q}})\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}\\
\tilde{\mathbf{q}}\end{pmatrix},

where the matrix
\mathbf{M}(\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{q}})
is given by, after an application of Hadamard’s Lemma,

\begin{pmatrix}-k_{p}\mathbf{I}-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\mathbf{p}+s\tilde{\mathbf{p}}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p}+s\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})ds&-\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\mathbf{q}+s\tilde{\mathbf{q}}}^{2}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}+s\tilde{\mathbf{q}})ds\\
\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\mathbf{p}+s\tilde{\mathbf{p}}}^{2}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p}+s\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\mathbf{q})ds&-k_{q}\mathbf{I}+\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\mathbf{q}+s\tilde{\mathbf{q}}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathcal{H}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\mathbf{q}+s\tilde{\mathbf{q}})ds\end{pmatrix}.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.1
, a sufficient condition for convergence of
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}(t)\rightarrow\mathbf{0}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{q}}(t)\rightarrow\mathbf{0}
is uniform negative definiteness of the Jacobian matrix

\mathbf{J}=\begin{pmatrix}-k_{p}\mathbf{I}-\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})&-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p})\\
\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}^{2}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})&-k_{q}\mathbf{I}+\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\end{pmatrix},

in
\mathbf{p}
and
\mathbf{q}
, i.e., contraction of the nominal
\dot{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}
and
\dot{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}
system in the Euclidean metric. Sufficient conditions for uniform negative definiteness are then given by (see, for example, Example 3.8 in
Slotine 2003
)

\displaystyle k_{p}

\displaystyle>-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})+\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right),

\displaystyle k_{q}

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})+\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right),

\displaystyle\lambda_{p}\lambda_{q}

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left[\nabla^{2}_{\mathbf{p}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})-\nabla^{2}_{\mathbf{q}}\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right],

where
\lambda_{p}
and
\lambda_{q}
are the contraction rates of the
\hat{\mathbf{p}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{q}}
systems respectively, given by the difference of the left- and right-hand sides of the first two inequalities above.

F.5
Proof of Proposition
4.3

The Euler-Lagrange equations of motion
\frac{d}{dt}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\dot{\mathbf{q}}}-\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\mathbf{q}}=\mathbf{u}
with
\mathbf{u}
a control input give the dynamics

\sum_{lj}a_{l}^{(K)}M_{ij}^{l}(\mathbf{q})\ddot{q}_{j}+\sum_{lkj}a_{l}^{(K)}\dot{q}_{k}\dot{q}_{j}\left[\frac{\partial M_{ij}^{l}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{k}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial M_{kj}^{l}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{i}}\right]+\sum_{l}a_{l}^{(P)}\frac{\partial\phi^{l}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{i}}=u_{i}.

Above, the second term

\sum_{lkj}a_{l}^{(K)}\dot{q}_{k}\dot{q}_{j}\left[\frac{\partial M_{ij}^{l}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{k}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial M_{kj}^{l}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{i}}\right]

uniquely defines the centripetal and Coriolis forces (traditionally written as
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})\dot{\mathbf{q}}
with
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})
the Coriolis matrix), but does not uniquely define the Coriolis matrix (see, e.g., Section 9.1 of
Slotine and Li 1991
). Choosing

C_{ij}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})=\sum_{kl}a_{l}^{(K)}\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{\partial M^{l}_{ij}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{k}}-\left(\frac{\partial M^{l}_{kj}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{i}}-\frac{\partial M^{l}_{ki}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{j}}\right)\right]\dot{q}_{k}

preserves the Coriolis force
\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})\dot{\mathbf{q}}
and ensures that
\dot{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbf{q})-2\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})
is a skew-symmetric matrix. In matrix notation, the dynamics are then given by

\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{q})\ddot{\mathbf{q}}+\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})\dot{\mathbf{q}}+\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{q})=\mathbf{u}

with the potential force
\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{q})=\sum_{l}a^{(P)}_{l}\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\phi^{l}(\mathbf{q})
. Defining
\mathbf{s}
and
\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{r}
as
\mathbf{s}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}},\mathbf{q}_{d}(t),\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{d}(t))=\left(\frac{d}{dt}+\lambda\right)\left(\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{q}_{d}(t)\right)=\dot{\mathbf{q}}-\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{r}
with
\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{r}=\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{d}(t)-\lambda\left(\mathbf{q}-\mathbf{q}_{d}(t)\right)
, these dynamics can be equivalently rewritten, dropping the arguments of
\mathbf{s}
for visual clarity,

\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{q})\dot{\mathbf{s}}+\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})\mathbf{s}=\mathbf{u}-\left(\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{q})\ddot{\mathbf{q}}_{r}+\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{r}+\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{q})\right).

(84)

Observe that because the Lagrangian was linearly parameterized, the resulting dynamics inherent a linear parameterization. Defining the
known
basis functions

\displaystyle Y^{(P)}_{il}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\phi^{l}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{i}},

\displaystyle Y^{(K)}_{il}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}},\mathbf{q}_{d}(t),\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{d}(t))

\displaystyle=\sum_{kj}\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{\partial M^{l}_{ij}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{k}}-\left(\frac{\partial M^{l}_{kj}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{i}}-\frac{\partial M^{l}_{ki}(\mathbf{q})}{\partial q_{j}}\right)\right]\dot{q}_{k}\dot{q}_{r,j}+\sum_{j}M_{ij}^{l}(\mathbf{q})\ddot{q}_{r,j},

we can write (
84
) as

\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{q})\dot{\mathbf{s}}+\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})\mathbf{s}=\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{Y}^{(P)}(\mathbf{q})\mathbf{a}^{(P)}-\mathbf{Y}^{(K)}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}},\mathbf{q}_{d}(t),\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{d}(t))\mathbf{a}^{(K)}.

For
\mathbf{K}>0
a constant positive definite matrix and for parameter estimates
\hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(P)}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(K)}
, taking
\mathbf{u}=-\mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{Y}^{(p)}(\mathbf{q})\hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(P)}+\mathbf{Y}^{(K)}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}},\mathbf{q}_{d}(t),\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{d}(t))\hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(K)}
leads to

\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{q})\dot{\mathbf{s}}+\mathbf{C}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}})\mathbf{s}=-\mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}+\mathbf{Y}^{(P)}(\mathbf{q})\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{(P)}+\mathbf{Y}^{(K)}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}},\mathbf{q}_{d}(t),\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{d}(t))\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{(K)}.

The proof in
Slotine and Li 1987
can now be directly extended. For
\psi^{(K)}
,
\psi^{(P)}
strongly convex functions and
\gamma_{K}>0
,
\gamma_{P}>0
positive gains, the Lyapunov-like function

V(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{q},\hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(K)},\hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(P)})=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{s}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{H}(\mathbf{q})\mathbf{s}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{K}}d_{\psi^{(K)}}\left(\mathbf{a}^{(K)}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(K)}\right)+\frac{1}{\gamma_{P}}d_{\psi^{(P)}}\left(\mathbf{a}^{(P)}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(P)}\right),

shows stability of the adaptation laws

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}^{(K)}

\displaystyle=-\gamma_{K}\nabla^{2}\psi^{(K)}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(K)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Y}^{(K)}(\mathbf{q},\dot{\mathbf{q}},\mathbf{q}_{d}(t),\dot{\mathbf{q}}_{d}(t))^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{s},

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}^{(P)}

\displaystyle=-\gamma_{P}\nabla^{2}\psi^{(P)}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}^{(P)}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{Y}^{(P)}(\mathbf{q})^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{s},

after an application of Barbalat’s Lemma (Lemma
C.1
) and using skew-symmetry of
\dot{\mathbf{H}}-2\mathbf{C}
to eliminate
\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{s}^{\mathsf{T}}\dot{\mathbf{H}}\mathbf{s}
.

F.6
Proof of Proposition
4.4

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\tilde{\mathbf{y}},\hat{\mathbf{a}})=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{y}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{y}}+\frac{1}{\gamma}d_{\psi}\left(\mathbf{a}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}\right).

Let
\frac{\partial\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{a}}{\partial\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y})
denote the derivative of
\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{y})\mathbf{a}
with respect to its argument evaluated at
\mathbf{y}
, and
\frac{\partial\mathbf{g}}{\partial\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2})
denote the derivative of
\mathbf{g}
with respect to its first argument evaluated at
(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2})
. Using that
\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y})=\mathbf{0}
for all
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, this Lyapunov-like function has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\tilde{\mathbf{y}},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbf{y}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{C}\left(\mathbf{Y}(\tilde{\mathbf{y}}+\mathbf{y}(t))\tilde{\mathbf{a}}+\left[\mathbf{Y}(\tilde{\mathbf{y}}+\mathbf{y}(t))-\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{y}(t))\right]\mathbf{a}+\mathbf{g}(\tilde{\mathbf{y}}+\mathbf{y}(t),\mathbf{y}(t))-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{y}(t),\mathbf{y}(t))\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tilde{\mathbf{y}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{Y}(\tilde{\mathbf{y}}+\mathbf{y}(t))\tilde{\mathbf{a}},

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbf{y}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{C}\left(\left[\mathbf{Y}(\tilde{\mathbf{y}}+\mathbf{y}(t))-\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{y}(t))\right]\mathbf{a}+\mathbf{g}(\tilde{\mathbf{y}}+\mathbf{y}(t),\mathbf{y}(t))-\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{y}(t),\mathbf{y}(t))\right),

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbf{y}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{C}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{a}}{\partial\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y}(t)+s\tilde{\mathbf{y}})+\frac{\partial\mathbf{g}}{\partial\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y}(t)+s\tilde{\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{y}(t))\right)ds\right)\tilde{\mathbf{y}},

where the last equality follows by Hadamard’s Lemma. As in the proof of Proposition
4.1
, this shows that a sufficient condition for convergence of
\tilde{\mathbf{y}}(t)\rightarrow 0
is

\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{C}\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{a}}{\partial\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y})+\frac{\partial\mathbf{g}}{\partial\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{u}(t)\right)+\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{a}}{\partial\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y})+\frac{\partial\mathbf{g}}{\partial\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{u}(t))\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{C}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\leq-\lambda\boldsymbol{\Gamma}

uniformly in
\mathbf{y}
for some contraction rate
\lambda>0
and any external input
\mathbf{u}(t)
. Under suitable observability assumptions on the system, convergence of
\hat{\mathbf{y}}(t)
to
\mathbf{y}(t)
ensures that
\hat{\mathbf{x}}(t)
converges to
\mathbf{x}(t)

(
Luenberger 1979
)
.

F.7
Proof of Proposition
4.5

The Lyapunov-like function

V(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}})=\frac{1}{\gamma}d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\Theta}\ \middle\|\ \widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\right),

has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}},t)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{ij}\widetilde{\Theta}_{ij}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{\Theta}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\right)_{i}x_{j}(t),

\displaystyle=-\sum_{ij}\widetilde{\Theta}_{ij}\left(\sigma_{i}\left(\sum_{k}\widehat{\Theta}_{ik}x_{k}(t)\right)-\sigma_{i}\left(\sum_{k}\Theta_{ik}x_{k}(t)\right)\right)x_{j}(t),

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i}\left(\sum_{k}\widetilde{\Theta}_{ik}x_{k}(t)\right)\left(\sigma_{i}\left(\sum_{k}\widehat{\Theta}_{ik}x_{k}(t)\right)-\sigma_{i}\left(\sum_{k}\Theta_{ik}x_{k}(t)\right)\right),

\displaystyle\leq-\sum_{i}\frac{1}{L_{i}}\left(\sigma_{i}\left(\sum_{k}\widehat{\Theta}_{ik}x_{k}(t)\right)-\sigma_{i}\left(\sum_{k}\Theta_{ik}x_{k}(t)\right)\right)^{2},

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{\max_{k}L_{k}}\left\|\boldsymbol{\sigma}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{\Theta}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 0.

Integrating the above inequality shows that
\left[\boldsymbol{\sigma}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{\Theta}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\right]
is an
\mathcal{L}_{2}
signal and hence that each component
\left[\sigma_{i}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)-\sigma_{i}\left(\boldsymbol{\Theta}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\right]
is also an
\mathcal{L}_{2}
signal. The error dynamics

\dot{\mathbf{e}}=-(k+1)\mathbf{e}+\boldsymbol{\sigma}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)-\boldsymbol{\sigma}\left(\boldsymbol{\Theta}\mathbf{x}(t)\right),

shows that each component
e_{i}
is a low-pass filter of each component of the function approximation error
\left[\sigma_{i}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)-\sigma_{i}\left(\boldsymbol{\Theta}\mathbf{x}(t)\right)\right]
. Applying Lemma
C.2
shows that
\mathbf{e}(t)\rightarrow\mathbf{0}
.

F.8
Proof of Proposition
6.1

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{v}},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)=Q(\mathbf{x},t)+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right).

(85)

Equation (
85
) implies that, with
\mathcal{N}(t)=1+\mu N(t)
,

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{v}},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)

\displaystyle=\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}N(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t),

\displaystyle\leq\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{a},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}N(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+2\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t),

\displaystyle\leq-\rho(Q(\mathbf{x},t))-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\leq 0.

(86)

By radial unboundedness of
Q(\mathbf{x},t)
in
\mathbf{x}
, (
85
) & (
86
) show that
\mathbf{x}
remains bounded. Similarly, radial unboundedness of
V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{v}},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
in
\tilde{\mathbf{v}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}
show that
\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t)
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)
remain bounded. Integrating (
86
) shows that
\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)-\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t)\|^{2}dt\leq V(\mathbf{x}(0),\mathbf{v}(0),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(0),0)<\infty
, so that
\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)-\hat{\mathbf{v}}(\cdot)\right)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
. An identical argument shows that
\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho\left(Q(\mathbf{x}(t),t)\right)dt<\infty
. Now, because
\mathbf{x}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}
are bounded, and because
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)
is locally bounded in
\mathbf{x}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}
uniformly in
t
by assumption, writing
\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(s)=\int_{s}^{t}\left(\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x}(t^{\prime}),\mathbf{a},t^{\prime})+\mathbf{u}(\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t^{\prime}),t^{\prime})\right)dt^{\prime}
shows that
\mathbf{x}(t)
is uniformly continuous in
t
. Because
Q(\mathbf{x},t)
is uniformly continuous in
t
when
\mathbf{x}
is bounded, because
Q(\mathbf{x}(t),t)
is bounded for all
t
, and because
\rho
is continuous in its argument, we conclude
\rho
is uniformly continuous in
t
and
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\rho(t)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\rho(Q(\mathbf{x}(t),t))=0
by Lemma
C.1
. This shows that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}Q(\mathbf{x}(t),t)=0
.
∎

F.9
Proof of Proposition
6.2

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\hat{\mathbf{v}},\hat{\mathbf{a}})=\frac{1}{2\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right).

(87)

Equation (
87
) implies that, with
\mathcal{N}(t)=1+\mu N(t)
,

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{v}},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}R(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}N(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}R(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t),

\displaystyle\leq R(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{a},t)-R(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}N(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}R(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t),

\displaystyle\leq-kR(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\leq 0.

(88)

(
87
) & (
88
) show boundedness of
\hat{\mathbf{v}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}
over
[0,T_{x}]
. Furthermore, integrating (
88
) shows that
\int_{0}^{T_{x}}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t^{\prime})-\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t^{\prime})\|^{2}dt^{\prime}<\infty
and
\int_{0}^{T_{x}}R(\mathbf{x}(t^{\prime}),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t^{\prime}),t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}<\infty
. For any bounded solution
\mathbf{x}(t)
, these integrals may be extended to infinity, and we conclude that
\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
,
\hat{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
, and
\hat{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
. Writing
\mathbf{x}(t)-\mathbf{x}(s)
in integral form as in the proof of Proposition
6.1
shows that
\mathbf{x}(t)
is uniformly continuous in
t
, and in light of the local boundedness assumption on
\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}R
, the same procedure can be applied to
\hat{\mathbf{v}}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}
. Because
R(\mathbf{x}(t),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t),t)
is uniformly continuous in
t
for bounded
\mathbf{x}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}
, and because
\mathbf{x}(t)
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)
are both uniformly continuous in
t
, we conclude that
R(\mathbf{x}(t),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t),t)
is uniformly continuous in
t
and
R(\mathbf{x}(t),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t),t)\rightarrow 0
by Barbalat’s Lemma (Lemma
C.1
).
∎

F.10
Proof of Proposition
6.3

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)=\frac{1}{2}s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}.

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
has time derivative

\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)=-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}\leq 0.

This immediately shows
s(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
and that
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
. Because
s(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
,
\mathbf{x}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
by definition of the sliding variable. Integrating
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
with respect to time shows that
s(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
and
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\mathbf{a},\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
. The result then follows by application of Lemma
C.2
or directly by Barbalat’s Lemma (Lemma
C.1
).
∎

F.11
Proof of Proposition
6.4

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov function

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)=\frac{1}{2}s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\left(\|\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\right).

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)

\displaystyle=-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}+s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{1}{\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(-\kappa\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)-\gamma s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\right)\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)+\gamma s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}+\kappa\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}+2\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\leq-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))|\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)\|+2\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}|\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)|\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)\|

\displaystyle\leq-\epsilon_{1}\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\epsilon_{2}\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\left(\sqrt{(1-\epsilon_{1})\eta}|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))|-\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1-\epsilon_{1})\eta}}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)\|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\left(\sqrt{\frac{(1-\epsilon_{2})\kappa}{\gamma}}|\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)|-\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\sqrt{\frac{\gamma}{(1-\epsilon_{2})\kappa}}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)\|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 0.

Above,
0<\epsilon_{1}<1
and
0<\epsilon_{2}<1
are arbitrary and we have taken
\mu=\frac{\gamma}{\beta}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\epsilon_{1})\eta}+\frac{\kappa}{(1-\epsilon_{2})\gamma}\right)
. Because
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
are arbitrary,
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
is negative semi-definite for
\mu>\frac{\gamma}{\beta}\left(\frac{1}{\eta}+\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\right)
. Hence
\hat{\mathbf{v}}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
,
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
, and
s(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
. Because
s(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
, we automatically have
\mathbf{x}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
, which shows that
\dot{s}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
by local boundedness of
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)
in
\mathbf{x}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}
uniformly in
t
. Integrating
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
with respect to time shows that
s(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot),\dot{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot),\dot{\mathbf{x}}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
and hence by Lemma
C.1
,
s(\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\rightarrow 0
and
\mathbf{x}(t)\rightarrow\mathbf{x}_{d}(t)
.
∎

F.12
Proof of Proposition
6.5

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}})=\frac{1}{2\gamma}\|\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}.

V(\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}})
has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(-\gamma\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)+\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)+\gamma\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\mathcal{N}(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+2\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}}{D_{1}}-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\|\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\||\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)|

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{D_{1}}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}-\beta\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\left(\sqrt{\frac{1-\epsilon}{D_{1}}}|\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)|-\sqrt{\frac{D_{1}}{1-\epsilon}}\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\|\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|\right)^{2}\leq 0.

Above,
0<\epsilon<1
is arbitrary and we have chosen
\mu=\frac{\gamma D_{1}}{(1-\epsilon)\beta}
. Because
\epsilon
is arbitrary, this shows that
\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t)
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)
remain bounded for
\mu>\frac{\gamma D_{1}}{\beta}
over the maximal interval of existence of
\mathbf{x}(t)
. By integrating
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
with respect to time shows that
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\mathbf{a},\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
over this same interval. Observe now that these bounds are independent of the length of the interval, as they depend only on the initial value of
V(\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}})
. Application of Lemma
C.2
completes the proof.
∎

F.13
Proof of Proposition
7.1

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},t)=Q(\mathbf{x},t)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}+\tilde{\overline{\mathbf{a}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}^{-1}\tilde{\overline{\mathbf{a}}}\right).

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},t)
has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},t)

\displaystyle=\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-k\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}^{-1}\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{a},t)-k\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}^{-1}\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\rho\left(\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)-k\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}^{-1}\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 0.

Because
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},t)\leq 0
and
V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},t)\geq 0
,
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)
and
\overline{\mathbf{a}}(t)
remain bounded. Radial unboundedness of
Q(\mathbf{x},t)
in
\mathbf{x}
ensures that
\mathbf{x}(t)
remains bounded. Integrating
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},t)
with respect to time shows that
\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)-\overline{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)\right)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
. The remainder of the argument follows the proof of Proposition
6.1
.
∎

F.14
Proof of Proposition
7.2

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{v}},t)=Q(\mathbf{x},t)+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\overline{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\tilde{\overline{\mathbf{v}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\overline{\mathbf{v}}}.

Observe that we can write:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(Q(\mathbf{x},t)+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)+\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)+\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{a},t)-\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)+\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)

Where the inequality follows by convexity of
\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
in its second argument. Now,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\left[\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right]+k_{a}\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\left[\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right]+\gamma\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-k_{v}\left[\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+\frac{k_{a}\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)+\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)

Summing the above, we find that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(Q(\mathbf{x},t)+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{a},t)+2\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)+\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+\frac{k_{a}\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)

Now, take
\mathcal{N}(t)=1+\mu N(t)
and write that

\displaystyle\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{a},t)+2\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\frac{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\leq-\rho(Q(\mathbf{x},t))-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

by Assumption
6.1
. Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(Q(\mathbf{x},t)+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\rho(Q(\mathbf{x},t))-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+\frac{k_{a}\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)

Consider

\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2\gamma}\tilde{\overline{\mathbf{v}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\overline{\mathbf{v}}}=\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\tilde{\overline{\mathbf{v}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\overline{\mathbf{v}}\right)

and then note that

\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\tilde{\overline{\mathbf{v}}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\overline{\mathbf{v}}\right)+\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)=-\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\|\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}.

The final term in the Lyapunov function satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\overline{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}=\frac{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\overline{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)-\frac{2k_{a}\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\|\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}

Putting all of these together,
V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{v}},t)
has a time derivative which satisfies

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{v}},t)

\displaystyle\leq-\rho(Q(\mathbf{x},t))-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\|\bar{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\left(k_{a}+1\right)\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)-\frac{k_{v}}{\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\overline{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)-\frac{2k_{a}\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\|\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\rho(Q(\mathbf{x},t))-\left(\frac{\beta}{\gamma}-\frac{k_{v}}{2\gamma}\right)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{k_{v}}{2\gamma}\|\bar{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\left(1-\frac{\epsilon(k_{a}+1)}{2}\right)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}{\gamma}\left(2k_{a}-\frac{k_{a}+1}{2\epsilon}\right)\|\overline{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}

Above,
\epsilon>0
is a small constant and the inequality follows by two applications of Young’s inequality. Clearly,
V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},\overline{\mathbf{a}},\overline{\mathbf{v}},t)\leq 0
if
\beta-\frac{k_{v}}{2}\geq 0
,
1-\frac{\epsilon(k_{a}+1)}{2}\geq 0
, and
2k_{a}-\frac{k_{a}+1}{2\epsilon}\geq 0
. The latter two inequalities lead to the requirement that
\frac{1}{4\epsilon-1}\leq k_{a}\leq\frac{2}{\epsilon}-1
. Taking the limit as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
shows that
k_{a}>0
can be arbitrary. The proof from here is identical to that of Proposition
6.1
.
∎

F.15
Proof of Proposition
8.1

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)=Q(\mathbf{x},t)+\frac{1}{\gamma}d_{\psi}\left(\mathbf{a}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right).

(89)

Equation (
89
) implies that, with
\mathcal{N}(t)=1+\mu N(t)
,

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)

\displaystyle=\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}N(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\mathbf{I}+\left[\nabla^{2}\psi(\hat{\mathbf{v}})\right]^{-1}\right)\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t),

\displaystyle\leq\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{a},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}N(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\mathbf{I}+\left[\nabla^{2}\psi(\hat{\mathbf{v}})\right]^{-1}\right)\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t),

\displaystyle\leq-\rho(Q(\mathbf{x},t))-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}.

(90)

The first line to the second follows by convexity of
\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
in its second argument, while the second line to the third follows by Assumption
8.1
. The remainder of the proof is identical to Proposition
6.1
.
∎

F.16
Proof of Proposition
8.2

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}})=\frac{1}{\gamma}d_{\psi}\left(\mathbf{a}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right).

(91)

Equation (
91
) implies that, with
\mathcal{N}(t)=1+\mu N(t)
,

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}R(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}N(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\mathbf{I}+\left[\nabla^{2}\psi(\hat{\mathbf{v}})\right]^{-1}\right)\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}R(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t),

\displaystyle\leq R(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{a},t)-R(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta\mu}{\gamma}N(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\mathbf{I}+\left[\nabla^{2}\psi(\hat{\mathbf{v}})\right]^{-1}\right)\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}R(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t),

\displaystyle\leq-kR(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)-\frac{\beta}{\gamma}\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}.

(92)

The first line to the second follows by convexity of
R(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
in its second argument, while the second to the third follows by Assumption
8.2
. The remainder of the proof is identical to Proposition
6.2
.
∎

Appendix G
Further results on dynamics prediction for Hamiltonian systems

We now provide some extensions to the results in Section
4.2
by exploiting the structure of separable Hamiltonians. With a separable Hamiltonian, it is natural to estimate the kinetic and potential energies separately,

\displaystyle T(\hat{\mathbf{p}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Y}_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{p}})\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{p},

\displaystyle U(\hat{\mathbf{q}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Y}_{q}(\hat{\mathbf{q}})\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{q},

where
\mathbf{Y}_{p}
and
\mathbf{Y}_{q}
are row vectors of basis functions for the kinetic and potential energies respectively. In this case, following the same derivation as in Section
4.1
, the error dynamics become

\displaystyle\dot{\tilde{\mathbf{p}}}

\displaystyle=-\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{Y}_{q}(\hat{\mathbf{q}})\tilde{\mathbf{a}}_{q}-k_{p}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}U(\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}U(\mathbf{q})\right),

\displaystyle\dot{\tilde{\mathbf{q}}}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathbf{Y}_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{p}})\tilde{\mathbf{a}}_{p}-k_{q}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}+\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}T(\hat{\mathbf{p}})-\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}T(\mathbf{p})\right).

Consider the adaptation laws

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}_{p}

\displaystyle=-\gamma_{p}\left[\nabla^{2}\psi_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{p})\right]^{-1}\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathbf{Y}_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{p}})\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{q}},

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}_{q}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{q}\left[\nabla^{2}\psi_{q}(\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{q})\right]^{-1}\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{Y}_{q}(\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{p}},

where
\psi_{p}(\cdot)
and
\psi_{q}(\cdot)
are strictly convex functions, and where
\gamma_{p}>0
and
\gamma_{q}>0
are positive learning rates. The Lyapunov-like function

V=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}+\frac{1}{\gamma_{p}}d_{\psi_{p}}\left(\mathbf{a}_{p}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}_{p}\right)+\frac{1}{\gamma_{q}}d_{\psi_{q}}\left(\mathbf{a}_{q}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}_{q}\right)

(93)

shows that a sufficient condition for convergence
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}\rightarrow 0
and
\tilde{\mathbf{q}}\rightarrow 0
is for the Jacobian

\mathbf{J}=\begin{pmatrix}-k_{p}\mathbf{I}&-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}U(\mathbf{q})\\
\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}^{2}T(\mathbf{p})&-k_{q}\mathbf{I}\end{pmatrix}

to be uniformly negative definite. A sufficient condition for uniform negative definiteness is given by (
30
).

While separable Hamiltonians encompass many physical systems, some, such as robotic systems, do not have this structure. A more general form encompassing robotic systems is

\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})=T(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})+U(\mathbf{q}).

Parameterizing these terms independently,

\displaystyle T(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Y}_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{p},

\displaystyle U(\hat{\mathbf{q}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Y}_{q}(\hat{\mathbf{q}})\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{q},

the error dynamics becomes

\displaystyle\dot{\tilde{\mathbf{p}}}

\displaystyle=-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{Y}_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)\tilde{\mathbf{a}}_{p}-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{Y}_{q}(\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)\tilde{\mathbf{a}}_{q}-k_{p}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}U(\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}U(\mathbf{q})\right)-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}T(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}T(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right),

\displaystyle\dot{\tilde{\mathbf{q}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathbf{Y}_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)\tilde{\mathbf{a}}_{p}-k_{q}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}+\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}T(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}T(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\right).

Now consider the adaptation laws

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}_{p}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{p}\left[\nabla^{2}\psi_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{p})\right]^{-1}\left(\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{Y}_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}-\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\mathbf{Y}_{p}(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{q}}\right),

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}_{q}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{q}\left[\nabla^{2}\psi_{q}(\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{q})\right]^{-1}\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\mathbf{Y}_{q}(\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{p}},

again where
\psi_{p}(\cdot)
and
\psi_{q}(\cdot)
are strictly convex functions, and where
\gamma_{p}>0
and
\gamma_{q}>0
are positive learning rates. The Lyapunov-like function (
93
) shows that a sufficient condition for convergence is for the Jacobian matrix

\mathbf{J}=\begin{pmatrix}-k_{p}\mathbf{I}-\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}T(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})&-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}U(\mathbf{q})-\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}T(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\\
\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}^{2}T(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})&-k_{q}\mathbf{I}+\nabla_{\mathbf{q}}\nabla_{\mathbf{p}}T(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})\end{pmatrix}

to be uniformly negative definite. Sufficient conditions for this are now given by

\displaystyle k_{p}

\displaystyle>-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}T(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})+\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}T(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right),

\displaystyle k_{q}

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}T(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})+\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}T(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right),

\displaystyle\lambda_{p}\lambda_{q}

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\max}^{2}\left[\nabla^{2}_{\hat{\mathbf{p}}}T(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla^{2}_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}T(\hat{\mathbf{p}},\hat{\mathbf{q}})-\nabla^{2}_{\hat{\mathbf{q}}}U(\hat{\mathbf{q}})\right],

similar to the fully general case handled in Sec.
4.2
. More general results can be obtained by using a non-Euclidean metric as a replacement for the momentum and position estimation error terms in (
93
).

G.1
Implicit regularization for higher-order laws

For simplicity, we only consider the linearly parameterized setting. The nonlinearly parameterized setting can be handled immediately.

Proposition G.1
.

Consider the velocity gradient algorithm with momentum

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{v}}}

\displaystyle=-\left[\nabla^{2}\psi(\hat{\mathbf{v}})\right]^{-1}\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t),

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}

\displaystyle=\beta\mathcal{N}(t)(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}).

Let
\psi:\mathbb{R}^{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a strongly convex function. Suppose that the unknown dynamics is linearly parameterized. Assume that
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)\rightarrow\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}\in\mathcal{A}
where
\mathcal{A}
is defined in (
22
). Then
\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}=\arg\min_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A}}d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{v}}(0)\right)
.
In particular, if
\hat{\mathbf{v}}(0)=\arg\min_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\psi(\boldsymbol{\theta})
, then

\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}=\arg\min_{\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A}}\psi(\boldsymbol{\theta}).

Proof.

First observe that if
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)\rightarrow\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}
, then
\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t)\rightarrow\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}
. To prove this fact, we will show that
\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}(t)\rightarrow 0
. consider

\|\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}(t)^{\mathsf{T}}\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}(t)dt=\int_{0}^{\infty}\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)-\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t)\|^{2}dt<\beta\sup_{t}\mathcal{N}(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}^{2}.

Note that
\sup_{t}\mathcal{N}(t)<\infty
because
\mathbf{x}(t)
remains bounded and because
\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)
is locally bounded in
\mathbf{x}
uniformly in
t
. Further note that
\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}<\infty
by Proposition
8.1
. Hence we conclude that
\|\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}}<\infty
. Moreover,
\|\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\infty}}<\infty
and
\|\ddot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\infty}}<\infty
by boundedness of
\hat{\mathbf{v}}
and boundedness of
\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x}(t),t)
. By Lemma
C.1
, then
\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}(t)\rightarrow 0
. Because
\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t)=\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)+\frac{\dot{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}(t)}{\beta\mathcal{N}(t)}
, this implies that
\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t)\rightarrow\hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}
.

Now let
\boldsymbol{\theta}
be any constant vector of parameters. The Bregman divergence has time derivative

\frac{d}{dt}d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)=-\left(\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\psi(\hat{\mathbf{v}})\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right).

Using that
\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\psi(\hat{\mathbf{v}})=-\nabla_{\hat{\mathbf{a}}}\dot{Q}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)=-\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\frac{\partial Q}{\partial\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{x},t)
and integrating both sides of the above shows

d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{v}}(0)\right)=d_{\psi}\left(\boldsymbol{\theta}\ \middle\|\ \hat{\mathbf{a}}_{\infty}\right)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial Q}{\partial\mathbf{x}}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\tau)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x}(\tau),\tau)\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}(\tau)-\boldsymbol{\theta}\right)d\tau.

Taking
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathcal{A}
, the integral becomes independent of
\boldsymbol{\theta}
. The proof from here is identical to the first-order case in Appendix
F.1
.
∎

G.2
Proof of Lemma
D.1

Proof.

Defining the vector
\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\hat{w}_{i}^{t}\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{x}_{i})
, (
72
) implies the iteration on
\hat{\mathbf{v}}
,

\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{t+1}=\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{t}-\frac{\lambda}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\hat{f}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{t},\mathbf{x}_{i})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i})\right)\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{x}_{i}).

(94)

(
94
) shows that at time
t
,

\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{t}=-\frac{\lambda}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}\tilde{f}_{i}^{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{t},\mathbf{x}_{i})\right)\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{x}_{i}),

(95)

where
\tilde{f}_{i}^{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{t},\mathbf{x}_{i})
in (
95
) is the function approximation error on the
i^{\text{th}}
input example at iteration
j
,
\tilde{f}_{i}^{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{t},\mathbf{x}_{i})=\hat{f}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{j},\mathbf{x}_{i})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i})
.

Now, assuming that for the adaptive control problem
f(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{a},t)=u(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathbf{x},t)\mathbf{a})
, setting
\mathbf{P}=\lambda\mathbf{I}
,
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(0)=\mathbf{0}
, and integrating both sides of (
12
), we see that at time
t
,

\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)=-\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(t^{\prime}),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t^{\prime}),\mathbf{a},t^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(t^{\prime}),t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}.

(96)

The current function approximation
\hat{f}(t)
at time
t
for the parameters in (
96
) can then be written

\displaystyle\hat{f}(t)

\displaystyle=u\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathbf{x},t)\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)\right)=u\left(\int_{0}^{t}-\lambda\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(t^{\prime}),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t^{\prime}),\mathbf{a},t^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathbf{x}(t),t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(t^{\prime}),t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=u\left(\int_{0}^{t}c(t^{\prime})\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\right)

(97)

where we have defined
c(t^{\prime})=-\lambda\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(t^{\prime}),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t^{\prime}),\mathbf{a},t^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{K}(t,t^{\prime})=\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathbf{x}(t),t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(t^{\prime}),t^{\prime})
. Similarly, in the case of the Alphatron, the current approximation at iteration
t
is given by

\displaystyle\hat{f}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{t},\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=u\left(\left\langle\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{t},\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{x})\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\right)=u\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}-\frac{\lambda}{m}\tilde{f}_{i}^{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{t},\mathbf{x}_{i})\right)\left\langle\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{x}),\boldsymbol{\phi}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=u\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\hat{w}_{i}^{t}\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{i})\right),

(98)

where we have noted that with
\hat{w}^{0}_{i}=0
for all
i
,
\hat{w}^{t}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}-\frac{\lambda}{m}\tilde{f}_{i}^{j}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{t},\mathbf{x}_{i})
.
∎

G.3
Proof of Proposition
E.1

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)=\frac{1}{2}s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}.

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
has time derivative

\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)=-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}-\frac{\lambda}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}\leq 0.

Negative semi-definiteness of
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
shows that
s(\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)
remain bounded. Because
s(\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))
remains bounded,
\mathbf{x}(t)
remains bounded. Integrating
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
with respect to time shows that
s(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
and
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\mathbf{a},\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
. The proof is completed by application of Lemma
C.2
or Lemma
C.1
.
∎

G.4
Proof of Proposition
E.2

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)=\frac{1}{2}s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right).

V(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)

\displaystyle=-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}+s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}+\tilde{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))+\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\right)\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{\lambda(t)}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(-\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)-\left(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))+\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\right)\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right]^{2}-\frac{\lambda(t)}{2}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}-2\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))+\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\right)\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{\lambda(t)}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)\right).

Now we use that

\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}=\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}+\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)

to say that

\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right]^{2}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)

\displaystyle=-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}-2s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}-\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}+\left[\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{\lambda(t)}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}-2s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}-\beta\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}-\beta\mu\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)\|^{2}\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\left[\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right]^{2}-\frac{\lambda(t)}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\eta s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}+2|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))|\|\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\|

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+|\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)|\|\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\|-\beta\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}-\left(\beta\mu-1\right)\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)\|^{2}\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{\lambda(t)}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\eta\epsilon_{1}s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))^{2}-\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{2}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}-\beta\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\left(\sqrt{(1-\epsilon_{1})\eta}|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))|-\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1-\epsilon_{1})\eta}}\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)\|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\left(\sqrt{\frac{1-\epsilon_{2}}{2}}|\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)|-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{2}{1-\epsilon_{2}}}\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|\|\mathbf{Y}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{\lambda(t)}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 0

Above,
0<\epsilon_{1}<1
and
0<\epsilon_{2}<1
are both arbitrary and we have chosen
\mu=\frac{1}{\beta}\left(1+\frac{1}{\eta(1-\epsilon_{1})}+\frac{1}{2(1-\epsilon_{2})}\right)
. Negative semi-definiteness of
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
shows that
s(\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))
,
\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t)
, and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)
remain bounded. Because
s(\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))
remains bounded,
\mathbf{x}(t)
remains bounded. Integrating
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
with respect to time shows that
s(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\mathbf{x}_{d}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
and
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\mathbf{a},\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
. By local boundedness of
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\mathbf{a},\cdot)
in
\mathbf{x}
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}
uniformly in
t
,
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(t),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t),\mathbf{a},t)
remains bounded and hence
\dot{s}(\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))
remains bounded. By Lemma
C.1
,
s(\mathbf{x}(t),\mathbf{x}_{d}(t))\rightarrow 0
and
\mathbf{x}(t)\rightarrow\mathbf{x}_{d}(t)
.
∎

G.5
Proof of Proposition
E.3

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}.

(99)

V(\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)

\displaystyle=-\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)^{2}-\frac{\lambda}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{a}},

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{D_{1}}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}+\frac{1}{2D_{2}^{2}}\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}=-\left(\frac{1}{D_{1}}-\frac{1}{2D_{2}^{2}}\right)\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)^{2}.

For
D_{1}<2D_{2}^{2}
,
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)\leq 0
. Integrating
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)
with respect to time then shows that
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\mathbf{a}(\cdot),\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
over the maximal interval of existence of
\mathbf{x}(t)
. Alternatively, using the same Lyapunov function,

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)

\displaystyle\leq-\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{2}\left(D_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right).

For
D_{2}>\frac{1}{2}
,
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},t)\leq 0
and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\cdot)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
over the maximal interval of existence of
\mathbf{x}(t)
. By Assumption
2.2
, this implies that
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\mathbf{a},\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
over this same interval. Hence, both approaches demonstrate that
\hat{\mathbf{a}}
remains bounded over the maximal interval of existence of
\mathbf{x}(t)
, and that
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\mathbf{a},\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
over the same interval. Furthermore, these bounds are independent of the length of the interval, as they only depend on
V(\hat{\mathbf{a}}(0),0)
. By Lemma
C.2
, the proposition is proved.
∎

G.6
Proof of Proposition
E.4

Proof.

Consider the Lyapunov-like function

V(\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)\right).

V(\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
has time derivative

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\left(-\lambda\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right)\tilde{\mathbf{v}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)+\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\left(-\lambda\mathbf{P}(t)^{-1}+\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\right)\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\left(\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+2\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)

\displaystyle\leq-D_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+2|\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\mathbf{a},t)|\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\|

Observe that

\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right]^{2}+\left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{a}}^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right)^{2}.

Then, rewrite

\displaystyle\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)

\displaystyle\leq-\left(D_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{2}+\left[\left(\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\right)^{\mathsf{T}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\beta\mathcal{N}(t)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}+\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\||\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}|\left(2D_{1}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\left(D_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{2}-\beta\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}-\left(\beta\mu-1\right)\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\||\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}|\left(2D_{1}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\epsilon\left(D_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}\right)^{2}-\beta\|\hat{\mathbf{a}}-\hat{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\left(\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)\left(D_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)}|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}|-\frac{2D_{1}+1}{2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)\left(D_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)}}\|\hat{\mathbf{v}}-\hat{\mathbf{a}}\|\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x},t)\|\right)^{2}.

Above,
0<\epsilon<1
is arbitrary and we have chosen
\mu=\frac{1}{\beta}\left(1+\frac{\left(2D_{1}+1\right)^{2}}{(1-\epsilon)(4D_{2}-2)}\right)
.
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
is clearly negative semi-definite for
D_{2}<\frac{1}{2}
, which shows that
\hat{\mathbf{v}}(t)
and
\hat{\mathbf{a}}(t)
remain bounded over the maximal interval of existence of
\mathbf{x}(t)
. Integrating
\dot{V}(\mathbf{x},\hat{\mathbf{a}},\hat{\mathbf{v}},t)
with respect to time shows that
\boldsymbol{\alpha}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\cdot)^{\mathsf{T}}\tilde{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
over this interval, which implies that
\tilde{f}(\mathbf{x}(\cdot),\hat{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot),\mathbf{a},\cdot)\in\mathcal{L}_{2}
over the same interval by Assumption
2.2
. Observe that the bounds are independent of the length of the interval, as they only depend on
V(\hat{\mathbf{a}}(0),\hat{\mathbf{v}}(0),0)
. By Lemma
C.2
, the proposition is proven.
∎
